Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Key findings:

1. **d025_v3_s42: NO constat files anywhere.** The `find` returned empty. The worker claimed to run s3_constat but no output was produced. This is a **failure with no artifact**.

2. **d000_v2_s42: 9 files now** — s1×3, s2×3, s3_constat×3. The constat files were written at 05:03 (about 2 hours ago). Previously this directory had 9/12 (missing s4×3). Now it's still 9/12 — the s4_selfcritique files are still missing. 

I should check on PID 630891 running on GPU 3 to see if it's still processing s4 for d000_v2_s42 on mmlu, and verify my count of the files in that directory — I'm seeing s1, s2, and s3_constat all complete with three datasets each, but s4_selfcritique hasn't produced anything yet.

Actually, that s4 computation is running on a deprecated alpha=16 checkpoint anyway, so even if it finishes, those results won't be used. More concerning is the worker_run_d025_v3_s3_constat_and_cuda_verify job that reported completion but didn't actually produce any s3_constat files for d025_v3_s42 — this looks like a silent failure where the worker claimed success but left no artifacts behind. The issue is likely that the s3_constat computation needs eval results that don't exist yet for the alpha=32 d025_v3 checkpoint, so the worker probably checked, found nothing, and exited without flagging the problem.

On the positive side, I've confirmed that d005_s123 and d000_s123 are both lora_alpha=32 checkpoints, which means the signals_v7 job running on cuda:2 is actually working with valid data.

The previous process on that GPU ran for 2.75 hours computing s4_selfcritique without producing any output files before dying, so there's a pattern of wasted compute here.       "content": "_next_id: 86\ndecisions:\n- id: D001\n  date: 2026-05-13 04:26 UTC\n  summary: MVP 用 simulation identifiability test 验证 4PL+γ IRT 核心假设，放弃 EMNLP 2026 May\n    ARR\n  rationale: 核心假设是 γ 与 θ 可分离——这在合成数据上可验证，无需 GPU。12 天内完成完整论文不现实，目标改为 AAAI 2027 / ICLR\n    2027。MVP 通过后再配置 GPU 进入 research 阶段。\n  related_experiments: []\n  depends_on: []\n- id: D002\n  date: 2026-05-13 04:29 UTC\n  summary: 采纳 Reviewer 3 个 warning：扩展 simulation grid + 加 θ-exposure confounding +\n    加 FPR/FNR 噪声模型\n  rationale: Reviewer 指出原 MVP simulation 条件过于理想化。θ-exposure confounding 是 γ identifiability\n    的核心威胁，必须在 MVP 阶段覆盖。参数 grid 扩展（18 conditions）和 FPR/FNR 噪声增量成本约 4-8h CPU，不影响时间线。\n  related_experiments: []\n  depends_on:\n  - D001\n- id: D003\n  date: 2026-05-13 10:57 UTC\n  summary: Phase A PASS：4PL+γ 在 N_items=300 下成功分离 γ 和 θ；θ_RMSE 阈值从 0.2 放宽至 0.30\n  rationale: 'Variant A (N_items=300, 4PL NUTS): γ_rel_bias=6.8%, θ_corr=0.943, 收敛完美。N_items=100\n    的 borderline fail 确认为信息量不足（c/d 参数估计需要更多 items），非方法论缺陷。θ_RMSE 阈值从 0.2 放宽至 0.30，Reviewer\n    论证理论最优 SE≈0.18-0.26，0.2 在 N=50 models 下不可达。ADVI 在 4PL 上彻底失败（bad local mode），MVP\n    阶段放弃 ADVI，仅用 NUTS。2PL+γ 在 4PL 生成数据上严重 misspecified（γ=0.16），确认 4PL 的必要性。'\n  related_experiments:\n  - exp_id: mvp_sim_4pl_gamma\n    role: primary\n    result: Phase A PASS (Variant A)\n  depends_on:\n  - D001\n  - D002\n- id: D004\n  date: 2026-05-13 15:45 UTC\n  summary: MVP 通过，进入 Research 阶段\n  rationale: Phase A (identifiability) 和 P2 (confounding robustness) 两个 gate 全部 PASS。4PL+γ\n    NUTS 在 N_items=300 条件下 γ 相对偏差 <8%，θ 相关性 >0.94，混淆分离后残差相关 0.058。ADVI 和 2PL 被排除。核心假设\"IRT\n    模型可分离污染效应与真实能力\"在合成数据上得到验证。\n  related_experiments:\n  - D002\n  - D003\n- id: D005\n  date: 2026-05-13 17:11 UTC\n  summary: Dream 审稿分析：best=5/10, median=5/10, worst=4/10。识别 6 个确认行动项，修改 framing +\n    plan，需 re-dream\n  rationale: Dream full review 暴露 6 个跨三档一致的结构性问题：(1) 单一全局 γ misspecified → 加 per-benchmark\n    γ_k 变体；(2) 引用不足 10 refs → 目标 30+；(3) Stage 1 uncertainty 未传播 → 加 measurement error\n    model 为默认；(4) 可识别性阈值无理论依据 → 加 Fisher Information 分析；(5) Signal survival 应为核心贡献\n    → 升级；(6) 单一模型家族 → 加 exp-014 multi-family。DCR baseline 此前已修复。RL regret ground truth\n    明确为 held-out clean benchmark。Local independence 加显式讨论。方向正确但执行需加强，不降 venue。\n  related_experiments:\n  - <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
OK, significant changes:

1. **New experiment registered**: `exp_001_sft_3B_d025_s42_a32_v2` — PENDING on cuda:3, PID 655064. This is the alpha=32 retrain of d025_s42. Good — this is useful work!

2. **exp_002_signals_3B_v5** now has `conclusion: "killed"` — the deprecated alpha=16 signal computation on cuda:4 (PID 648627) was marked as killed. But PID 648627 is still showing in GPU compute apps! Let me check.
   
3. GPU 3 is now double-booked with two processes running simultaneously: the old deprecated d000_v2_s42 signal computation at 8694 MiB and the new d025_s42_a32_v2 training at 12336 MiB, consuming 85.6% of the GPU's memory. The worker appears to have submitted the new training job without properly cleaning up the stale process first. while the deprecated signal computation (PID 654561) is still running on the same GPU. This mirrors the cuda:0 issue I flagged earlier.

Wait, but the D084 decision says "kill orphan + submit signals". The worker was supposed to kill the orphan and then submit. But PID 654561 (the deprecated s4 computation) is still running alongside the new training process.

Let me verify:
- GPU 3 UUID is GPU-c30258a6
- PID 654561: 8694 MiB — this is the d000_v2_s42 s4 signal (deprecated)
- PID 655069: 12336 MiB — this is the new d025_s42_a32_v2 training
- Total: 21030 MiB

This is tight but not as extreme as cuda:0 (23258 MiB). But running deprecated + new useful work on the same GPU is both wasteful and risky.

Actually, I'm realizing the worker `worker_ops_d084-kill-orphan-and-submit-signals` was targeting a different orphan—exp_002_signals_3B_v6 (DONE/failed, previously on cuda:3). The current PID 654561 came from an earlier worker setup, so the kill command may have missed it entirely, and the new submission went through without noticing the stale process was still consuming resources. Meanwhile, exp_002_signals_3B_v5 shows conclusion=killed in the registry but PID 648627 is still active on GPU 4, suggesting the registry update didn't actually terminate the process.

I need to check if PID 648627 is still alive and understand whether we have a registry-process mismatch here.
```

```text
Confirmed:

**GPU 3: DOUBLE-BOOKED AGAIN**
- PID 654561 (8694 MiB): deprecated d000_v2_s42 s4 signal (alpha=16), running 144 seconds
- PID 655069 (12336 MiB): NEW d025_a32_s42 training (alpha=32), running 55 seconds
- Total: 21052/24564 MiB (85.7%), 100% GPU util
- Only ~3.5GB headroom

This is problematic:


The old signal process wasn't terminated before launching the new training job, so both are now fighting for GPU 3 at full utilization. Since the training just started, I haven't seen performance degradation yet, but it's only a matter of time.

**GPU 4: exp_002_signals_3B_v5 marked "killed" but process still alive**
- PID 648627 still running (2762 seconds, 149% CPU, 7526 MiB)
- Registry says conclusion="killed" for v5
- But the actual process was NOT killed! Registry/process state mismatch
- This is computing deprecated alpha=16 d

There's a state mismatch here—the registry thinks v5 is dead, but the process is still actively running and consuming resources. The process needs to actually be terminated.

Looking at the full picture across all GPUs, I'm seeing two separate cleanup failures: GPU 3 has the deprecated signal and new training competing, while GPU 4 has a process that the registry marked as killed but never actually terminated. Both stem from the worker updating the registry without properly killing the underlying processes. I should document these issues in a report.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Reviewer for the `contamcal-irt-calibration` project (IRT-based benchmark contamination calibration research targeting EMNLP 2026 ARR May 25). My role is to audit experiment conclusions, verify data integrity, check GPU compliance, and report findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. I operate read-only and review worker completions and cron notifications as they arrive. The session involved reviewing ~10 worker completions, performing GPU resource audits, verifying checkpoint alpha values, and posting 4 review reports to the Director.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **IRT 4PL+γ model**: Core method for separating contamination effects (γ) from true ability (θ)
   - **LoRA SFT**: Fine-tuning with LoRA (r=16, alpha=32 standard per D055), lr=5e-5 (per D076)
   - **Alpha=32 standardization (D055)**: All experiments standardized to alpha=32; 5 old 3B s42 experiments had alpha=16 and need retraining
   - **D076 retrain**: 3B s42 models being retrained with lr=5e-5 (old lr=2e-4 was a confound)
   - **Contamination dosage levels**: d000 (0%), d005 (5%), d025 (25%), d100 (100%)
   - **Signal pipeline**: s1_embedding, s2_minkpp, s3_constat (orig-para accuracy gap), s4_selfcritique
   - **s123 checkpoints confirmed alpha=32**: d005_s123 and d000_s123 both have lora_alpha=32, valid for IRT pooling
   - **s42 deprecated checkpoints confirmed alpha=16**: d100_s42 and d000_v2_s42 both have lora_alpha=16
   - **Catastrophic forgetting signal (D085)**: 1.5B GSM8K accuracy decreases with dosage (d000=0.592 → d100=0.531) while MMLU/ARC increase — contamination SFT squeezes math reasoning
   - **Server**: cqa1-21977 with 6× NVIDIA RTX 4090D (24GB each)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **Registry (registry.yaml)**: Central experiment tracking. Key RUNNING experiments at session end:
     - exp_001_sft_3B_d005_s42_a32 (cuda:0, PID 643167, alpha=32 retrain)
     - exp_001_eval_3B_d100_s123_a32_batch (cuda:0, PID 642763, eval — double-booked with above)
     - exp_001_sft_3B_d100_s42_a32_v3 (cuda:1, PID 628668, alpha=32 retrain)
     - exp_002_signals_3B_v7 (cuda:2, PID 652259, d005_s123 signals — valid alpha=32)
     - exp_001_sft_3B_d025_s42_a32_v2 (cuda:3, PID 655069, NEW alpha=32 retrain — double-booked with deprecated signal PID 654561)
     - exp_001_sft_3B_d000_s42_a32 (cuda:5, PID 633041, alpha=32 retrain)
     - exp_004_sim_sweep_v7 (local server, IRT simulation)
   
   - **Checkpoint adapter_config.json files verified**:
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/checkpoints/Qwen2.5-3B_d100_s42/lora_adapter/adapter_config.json`: lora_alpha=16 (deprecated)

   - **Signal output directories**:
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp002/signals/Qwen2.5-3B_d025_v3_s42/`: 6/12 files (s1×3, s2×3, NO s3_constat, NO s4)
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp002/signals/Qwen2.5-3B_d000_v2_s42/`: 9/12 files (s1×3, s2×3, s3×3, NO s4 — s4 processes repeatedly failed to produce output)
     - `/root/autodl-tmp/exp002/signals/Qwen2.5-3B_d005_s123/`: 1/12 (s1_embedding_mmlu only, v7 still computing)
   
   - **decisions.yaml** (read offset 850-948):
     - D048/D049/D050: LoRA alpha=32 standardization decisions
     - D051: Accept 3B s123 alpha=16, don't rerun; compare after s42 alpha=32 retrain (BUT actual verification showed s123 IS alpha=32, so D051's premise was incorrect — s123 was never alpha=16)
     - D083: SIGTERM root cause confirmed — Worker agent itself sent SIGTERM via ssh_execute
     - D084: cuda:3 orphan kill + cuda:0 don't intervene + cuda:2/3 prioritize signals
     - D085: 1.5B GSM8K catastrophic forgetting confirmed, will include in paper

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **GPU 0 double-booking**: eval (PID 642763, 8976 MiB) + training (PID 643167, 14268 MiB) simultaneously on cuda:0. Total 23258/24564 MiB. Training step time degraded from ~4.5s/it to ~5.7s/it (+28%). D084 decided not to intervene since eval will finish first. Reported as warning.
   
   - **GPU 3 & 4 deprecated alpha=16 signal computations**: Multiple processes computing signals on deprecated checkpoints (d000_v2_s42 alpha=16, d100_s42 alpha=16). Flagged repeatedly across 4 reports. Director created D084 to address but cleanup was incomplete.
   
   - **GPU 3 double-booking (NEW)**: Worker submitted d025_s42_a32_v2 training (PID 655069, 12336 MiB) to cuda:3 without killing existing deprecated signal process (PID 654561, 8694 MiB). Total 21052/24564 MiB. Reported as CRITICAL — OOM risk imminent based on D083 precedent.

Reported as warning.

Confirmed as snapshot artifact — subsequent check showed 40-50% util, training progressing normally at 78% (2446/3125). Step time did degrade from ~3.9s/it to ~5.0s/it, possibly I/O contention.
